                    The correct answer is 3) A is true, but R is false.

                    \n\n

                    Key Points

                    \n\n
                      \n
                    • Assertion (A): The murals in Tirumalaipuram and Sittanavasal caves are important surviving examples of Pandya art. This statement is true.
                      • \n
                        \n
                      • The Sittanavasal cave paintings, located in Tamil Nadu, are one of the finest examples of early Pandya art and culture.
                        • \n
                      • These paintings primarily depict scenes of daily life, animals, and lotus ponds, showcasing the artistic skills of the Pandya period (7th century CE).
                        • \n
                      • The murals in these caves were created using the fresco-secco technique, which involves painting on dry plaster.
                        • \n
                      • Similarly, the Tirumalaipuram caves also hold significant artistic value, with inscriptions and carvings attributed to the Pandya dynasty.
                        • \n
                      \n
                    • Reason (R): These murals are mainly found on the outer walls of temples and depict mythological war scenes. This statement is false.
                      • \n
                        \n
                      • The murals in Sittanavasal caves are not located on the outer walls but rather on the inner walls and ceilings of the caves.
                        • \n
                      • The artwork mostly portrays Jain themes, such as scenes from Jain mythology, lotus ponds, and depictions of ascetics, rather than war scenes.
                        • \n
                      • The paintings focus on themes of spirituality, nature, and peace, consistent with Jainism's emphasis on non-violence and meditation.
                        • \n
                      \n
                    \n\n

                    Additional Information

                    \n\n
                      \n
                    • Sittanavasal Caves: \n
                        \n
                      • Located in Tamil Nadu, these caves date back to the 7th century CE.
                        • \n
                      • The murals represent the Jain religion and are a testament to the Pandya dynasty's contribution to art and culture.
                        • \n
                      • The paintings are made using natural pigments such as red ochre, black, yellow, and green.
                        • \n
                      • The lotus pond is a recurring theme in the murals, symbolizing prosperity and purity.
                        • \n
                      \n
                      • \n
                    • Murals and Artistic Techniques: \n
                        \n
                      • The fresco-secco technique used in these caves involves painting on dry plaster, unlike the fresco technique, which is done on wet plaster.
                        • \n
                      • The paintings show a high level of skill in shading, detailing, and depicting natural elements.
                        • \n
                      \n
                      • \n
                    • Pandya Dynasty:\n
                        \n
                      • The Pandya dynasty ruled parts of southern India, particularly Tamil Nadu, and were great patrons of art and architecture.
                        • \n
                      • They supported Jainism and built several Jain temples and monasteries during their reign.
                        • \n
                      \n
                      • \n
                    • Key Themes of Sittanavasal Paintings:\n
                        \n
                      • The murals primarily depict Jain monks engaged in meditation and scenes from nature, emphasizing simplicity and tranquility.
                        • \n
                      • Floral and faunal motifs such as lotuses, fish, and birds are commonly seen in the artwork.
                        • \n
                      \n
                      • \n
                    \n\n

                    Important Points

                    \n\n
                      \n
                    • The Sittanavasal paintings are among the few surviving examples of ancient Jain art in South India.
                      • \n
                    • These paintings are often compared to the Ajanta murals in terms of their artistic excellence, though they are less well-preserved.
                      • \n
                    • The caves are a protected monument under the care of the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI).

                      The correct answer is 2 only.

                      \n\n

                      Key Points

                      \n\n
                        \n
                      • Hooghly Region: This is a major industrial area in India, primarily located in West Bengal. It includes industries like jute, textiles, engineering, and chemicals along the Hooghly River. Hence, this is a major industrial region.
                        • \n
                      • Kolhapur-South Kannada Region: This region is not considered a major industrial area compared to other regions in India. While it has industries, its scale and contribution to India's industrial output are relatively limited. Hence, this is NOT a major industrial region.
                        • \n
                      • Gujarat Region: Gujarat is one of India's most prominent industrial hubs, with major industries like petrochemicals, textiles, pharmaceuticals, and diamond cutting. Hence, this is a major industrial region.
                        • \n
                      • Vishakhapatnam-Guntur Region: Located in Andhra Pradesh, this region is a significant industrial center with industries like steel, shipbuilding, fertilizers, and chemicals. Hence, this is a major industrial region.
                        • \n
                      • Durg-Raipur Region: Situated in Chhattisgarh, this region is known for its steel and iron industries and is a key industrial area in central India. Hence, this is a major industrial region.
                        • \n
                      \n\n

                      Additional Information

                      \n\n
                        \n
                      • Hooghly Industrial Region:\n
                          \n
                        • Located in West Bengal, this region is known for jute manufacturing, textiles, and engineering industries.
                          • \n
                        • It benefits from proximity to Kolkata, easy access to ports, and abundant labor.
                          • \n
                        • Major industries include jute mills, paper mills, chemicals, and engineering products.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      • Gujarat Industrial Region:\n
                          \n
                        • Gujarat is one of the fastest-growing industrial regions in India, with cities like Ahmedabad, Surat, and Vadodara leading the way.
                          • \n
                        • Key industries include petrochemicals, pharmaceuticals, textiles, and diamond processing.
                          • \n
                        • The state is well-known for its SEZs (Special Economic Zones) and ports.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      • Vishakhapatnam-Guntur Region:\n
                          \n
                        • This region is an important industrial hub in Andhra Pradesh, with major industries including steel plants, shipbuilding, and chemical production.
                          • \n
                        • Vishakhapatnam is home to India's largest shipyard and significant defense industries.
                          • \n
                        • It also has significant contributions to agriculture-based industries like fertilizers.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      • Durg-Raipur Industrial Region:\n
                          \n
                        • This region in Chhattisgarh is primarily known for its steel and iron industries.
                          • \n
                        • It houses the Bhilai Steel Plant, one of India's largest steel manufacturers.
                          • \n
                        • Other industries include cement, aluminum, and power generation.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      \n\n

                      Important Points

                      \n\n
                        \n
                      • Industrial Regions in India:\n
                          \n
                        • Industrial regions are formed due to factors like resource availability, transportation facilities, skilled labor, and proximity to markets.
                          • \n
                        • India's industrial regions are concentrated in states like Maharashtra, Gujarat, Tamil Nadu, West Bengal, and Karnataka.
                          • \n
                        • Examples of major regions include Mumbai-Pune, Ahmedabad-Vadodara, Chennai-Coimbatore, and Delhi-Faridabad.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      • Role of Industrial Regions:\n
                          \n
                        • Industrial regions contribute significantly to India's GDP and employment.
                          • \n
                        • They act as hubs for innovation, technology, and manufacturing.
                          • \n
                        • These regions often facilitate international trade due to their proximity to ports and airports.
                          • \n
                        \n
                        • \n
                      • Kolhapur-South Kannada Region:\n
                          \n
                        • While this region has industries, it is not as prominent or developed as other major industrial hubs in India.
                          • \n
                        • Industries in this region are smaller in scale and focus on local and regional markets.
                          • \n

                        Key Points

                        \n\n
                          \n
                        • हुगली क्षेत्र: यह भारत का एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है, जो मुख्य रूप से पश्चिम बंगाल में स्थित है। इसमें हुगली नदी के किनारे जूट, वस्त्र, इंजीनियरिंग और रसायन जैसे उद्योग शामिल हैं। अतः, यह एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है।
                          • \n
                        • कोल्हापुर-दक्षिण कन्नड़ क्षेत्र: भारत के अन्य क्षेत्रों की तुलना में इस क्षेत्र को एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र नहीं माना जाता है। यद्यपि इसमें उद्योग हैं, लेकिन भारत के औद्योगिक उत्पादन में इसका पैमाना और योगदान अपेक्षाकृत सीमित है। अतः, यह एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र नहीं है।
                          • \n
                        • गुजरात क्षेत्र: गुजरात भारत के सबसे प्रमुख औद्योगिक केंद्रों में से एक है, जिसमें पेट्रोकेमिकल्स, वस्त्र, फार्मास्यूटिकल्स और हीरा कटिंग जैसे प्रमुख उद्योग हैं। अतः, यह एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है।
                          • \n
                        • विशाखापत्तनम-गुंटूर क्षेत्र: आंध्र प्रदेश में स्थित, यह क्षेत्र इस्पात, जहाज निर्माण, उर्वरक और रसायन जैसे उद्योगों के साथ एक महत्वपूर्ण औद्योगिक केंद्र है। अतः, यह एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है।
                          • \n
                        • दुर्ग-रायपुर क्षेत्र: छत्तीसगढ़ में स्थित, यह क्षेत्र अपने इस्पात और लौह उद्योगों के लिए जाना जाता है और मध्य भारत में एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है। अतः, यह एक प्रमुख औद्योगिक क्षेत्र है।
                          • \n
                        \n\n

                        Additional Information

                        \n\n
                          \n
                        • हुगली औद्योगिक क्षेत्र:\n
                            \n
                          • पश्चिम बंगाल में स्थित यह क्षेत्र जूट निर्माण, वस्त्र और इंजीनियरिंग उद्योगों के लिए जाना जाता है।
                            • \n
                          • यह कोलकाता के निकटता, बंदरगाहों तक आसान पहुँच और प्रचुर श्रम से लाभान्वित होता है।
                            • \n
                          • प्रमुख उद्योगों में जूट मिलें, पेपर मिलें, रसायन और इंजीनियरिंग उत्पाद शामिल हैं।
                            • \n
                          \n
                          • \n
                        • गुजरात औद्योगिक क्षेत्र:\n
                            \n
                          • गुजरात भारत के सबसे तेजी से बढ़ते औद्योगिक क्षेत्रों में से एक है, जिसमें अहमदाबाद, सूरत और वडोदरा जैसे शहर अग्रणी हैं।
                            • \n
                          • प्रमुख उद्योगों में पेट्रोकेमिकल्स, फार्मास्यूटिकल्स, वस्त्र और हीरा प्रसंस्करण शामिल हैं।
                            • \n
                          • यह राज्य अपने SEZs (विशेष आर्थिक क्षेत्रों) और बंदरगाहों के लिए जाना जाता है।

                            The correct answer is It has received Presidential assent.

                            \n\n

                            Key Points

                            \n\n
                              \n\t
                            • In India, the Concurrent List refers to subjects on which both the Central and State governments can legislate, as per the Seventh Schedule of the Constitution.
                              • \n\t
                            • When there is a conflict between a Central law and a State law on a matter in the Concurrent List, the Central law generally prevails under Article 254(1) of the Constitution.
                              • \n\t
                            • However, if a State law that is inconsistent with a Central law has been passed and subsequently receives Presidential assent, it will prevail in that particular state under Article 254(2). This is an exception to the general rule.
                              • \n\t
                            • The Presidential assent allows the State law to override the Central law, but the overriding effect is limited to the territory of that specific state.
                              • \n\t
                            • This provision reflects the federal nature of India's governance, allowing states some flexibility in dealing with matters where there may be unique regional requirements.
                              • \n
                            \n\n

                            Additional Information

                            \n\n
                              \n\t
                            • Concurrent List under the Indian Constitution:\n\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • The Concurrent List is one of the three lists under the Seventh Schedule of the Constitution, alongside the Union List and the State List.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Subjects in the Concurrent List include matters such as education, marriage and divorce, bankruptcy, forests, and criminal law, where both the Center and States can legislate.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • This list allows for cooperative federalism, ensuring both levels of government can address issues of national and regional importance.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n\t
                            • Presidential Assent:\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • Presidential assent is the approval given by the President of India for a bill passed by a state legislature that conflicts with an existing Central law.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Under Article 254(2), once a state bill receives Presidential assent, it can prevail within that specific state even if it is inconsistent with the Central law.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • This mechanism ensures a balance between the autonomy of states and the overarching authority of the Central government.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n\t
                            • Article 254 of the Constitution:\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • Article 254(1): If there is a conflict between laws made by the Center and the State on a Concurrent List subject, the Central law will prevail.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Article 254(2): A State law that conflicts with a Central law can prevail in the state if it receives Presidential assent, but this does not affect the validity of the Central law in other parts of the country.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • This provision highlights India's quasi-federal structure, where states have limited autonomy in legislative matters.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n\t
                            • Examples of State Override:\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • One notable example is the Maharashtra Animal Preservation Act, which received Presidential assent and prevails over Central laws related to animal preservation in Maharashtra.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Such instances underscore the importance of Presidential assent in addressing regional legislative priorities.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n
                            \n\n

                            Important Points

                            \n\n
                              \n\t
                            • Role of the President:\n\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • The President's role in granting assent is crucial as it ensures the constitutional validity of laws enacted by state legislatures that may conflict with Central laws.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • By granting assent, the President upholds the principles of federalism while ensuring the legislative needs of individual states are met.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n\t
                            • Union vs State Legislation:\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • The Union List contains subjects exclusively legislated by the Center, while the State List is reserved for state legislatures.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • The Concurrent List serves as a shared domain, enabling both levels of government to legislate on crucial subjects.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Conflicts are resolved through mechanisms like Presidential assent or judicial review.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n\t
                            • Judicial Interpretation:\n\t
                                \n\t\t
                              • The judiciary plays a significant role in interpreting Article 254 and resolving disputes related to legislative conflicts.
                                • \n\t\t
                              • Several landmark cases have clarified the scope and application of state laws that override Central laws with Presidential assent.
                                • \n\t
                              \n\t
                              • \n
